# phase-b — the ralph queue (RUNNING since 2026-09-26, phase-b-3)

Designed by the seat with the operator on 2026-09-25 (ralph/decisions/phase-b-1.md).
It replaces the single plan-and-mint row staged on 2026-09-24 (five-programs-39):
the rows below are OUTCOMES (ralph/PROMPT.base.md §2), each with its own proof, and
there is no mint step. Nothing runs this queue until the operator launches it at
five-programs' `HUMAN-phase-b` row. Launch line: this directory's queue.toml header.

What Phase B is: making every program takeable ALONE. "The next developer who wants
THIS but not THAT" (operator, 2026-09-25). It builds the owners the five-programs
exceptions stood in for, and it never adds one. Design: docs/FIVE_PROGRAMS.md §1,
§2 (six programs: `serve` is split out of `cmnwlth`), §2c (distributions, the host
kit, the compose rule), §4 rules 6-7, and §12 3a (the ladder, with the mechanism
rung). Finish: FIVE_PROGRAMS §12 "Done".
- boundary-gate exits 0;
- no `package = "svrn"` exception remains;
- all six lift sandboxes pass;
- ~~each §2c drive has one implementation~~ — to phase-c (operator, 2026-09-27, phase-b-32). A row still never ADDS a second copy of a drive; collapsing an existing one is phase-c's.

**Scope guard (operator, 2026-09-27, phase-b-29).** Every row names the finish item it
advances: a violation or `svrn` exception it retires, a lift it makes pass, or a
pre-registered bar it measures (§2c copy counts left the finish in phase-b-32). Work that advances none of them
goes to ralph/next/phase-c/. A seat note or a director rewrite asks only for what the
row's own outcome and proof already require, or for what an ARCH principle the diff
itself breaks requires; anything else goes to the row that owns it or to phase-c, never
onto the row in flight. A row whose census finds more than twice its stated LIFT is
split or folded before any code.


**Scope is frozen (operator, 2026-09-27, phase-b-32: "We can't keep adding scope").** The open rows at
phase-b-32 are Phase B, listed in ralph/next/phase-b/scope.txt, which queue.toml's `scope_file` makes the planner
enforce: a row outside it waits on the operator and never dispatches. A split of an in-scope row keeps its parent's
id as a prefix (`<id>-<suffix>`) and its parent's scope; audit rows are in scope. A finding that is not needed to
close a row's own edge, exception or lift goes to ralph/next/phase-c/ by default, or parks if it truly blocks. The
burn-down is counted in edges (49 at phase-b-32), exceptions (4) and failing lifts, never in rows. End-user quality
is not part of the cut: the lane bars, pb-rails-untether and pb-rails-idle stay.



**Host steps (operator, 2026-09-27, phase-b-34: "You may restart the deployed daemon, migrate ~/.svrnmesh/notes.db
behind a backup, and edit the live mesh roster").** Those three, and a long measurement run detached under the loop's
`waiting` marker, are the rows' own last steps, not HUMAN rows: pb-code-daemon-exit restarts, pb-notes-memory migrates
behind the copy its code takes, pb-mesh-exit-transport cleans the roster, pb-serve-distributes runs its bar detached.
Each names how it is undone. Nothing else on the live host is granted: phase-b-7's "a worker never restarts it" still
holds for every other row.

**Premises after the sweep (phase-b-33).** REVIEW-pb-preflight-4 (the seat's three-agent sweep, 2026-09-27) re-checked
every open row at HEAD and rewrote each false premise it found. Two rules follow from what it found. A cite into a
file that an earlier row in the same chain replaced or moved is re-found by symbol; it is not a false premise and
not a halt. A compile trial never proves a crate's test targets (a failing lib stops `--keep-going` before its
`tests/`), so every trial's count is completed by `grep -rl '<dep>::' <crate>/tests`, and a row's census names those
sites too.

**Census before code (phase-b-29).** No row reaches a worker without a use-site census:
the call sites of every symbol it moves; a trial of the move (the refactor-apply recipe
applied, then COMPILE, LAYER and BOUNDARY, then reverted, the result pasted into the
row); and the runtime traffic crossing the moved symbols (who listens, readiness, which
fields cross a wire, which process owns a lifecycle). A premise written from memory or
from the gate's edge list is not a census. REVIEW-pb-preflight-1..3 do this for every
open row at once, and end in ONE package of the remaining operator forks. phase-b-30 ran
the pre-flight in the seat's session; every work row carries a `finish:` bullet and a
`trial:` bullet, and queue.toml's `dispatch_requires` makes the planner refuse a row
without them.

Scope (phase-b-2, 2026-09-25): only the rows the finish needs. Six rows that close no red edge and make no lift pass moved to the staged follow-on queue ralph/next/phase-c/, with their design kept: the Url venue, the inference origin, bench's three dials, the contracts re-home, the provider split and the per-program config files. Mechanical moves use `cargo xtask refactor-apply`. Each program's "runs alone" proof is its RUN smoke in `scripts/program-lift.sh`. Dead code is deleted before anything ports it. The svrn daemon adopts the shared shell and MCP dispatcher LAST, after it has shrunk.

Order: collapse before split (principle 8). The host kit, the one MCP dispatcher
and the one serving assembly land before any host is stood up. Hosts come before
their clients. Every row names the owner it extends; the appendix maps every red
edge to exactly one row.

**Pre-registered bars (set 2026-09-25, before any data; principle 7).** They apply
to pb-svrn-dials-serve. Its worker measures the two latency bars before the switch, on temp
roots. HUMAN-pb-lanes-dials-serve measures the two lane bars on the deployed daemon after the switch,
because the lanes read the daemon on :9741 and a worker never restarts it (phase-b-7). The bars are unchanged.
- Serving over loopback vs in-process, n ≥ 5 runs each, same host, same model:
  first-token latency p50 at most 10% slower, and embedding throughput at batch 32
  at least 90% of in-process.
- `svrn quality check --lane retrieval-prod` and the synth lane stay inside their
  noise bands (sovereign/docs/RUNBOOK.md §6).
- Profile (operator, 2026-09-26, phase-b-25, set before any release-profile data):
  the two latency bars bind cargo's `release` profile, as a distribution builds it.
  Debug readings (what this host deploys) are reported beside them and gate nothing.

A miss is NEEDS_HUMAN with the numbers, never a re-tuned bar.

- [x] pb-handover-first 075beac8a — depends [] — OUTCOME: an existing install's first boot at HEAD serves the ring history it already had (phase-b-3). On a copy of the operator's `~/.svrnmesh/rings` (10 namespaces, 14 MB), the HEAD daemon brought cw-rails up at boot (daemon_cmd/boot.rs:469 `ensure_rails`, fp-solo-clients). cw-rails logged `kv: rebuilt the store from the journals on disk namespaces=0` 380 ms BEFORE `start_daemon`'s one-time handover (daemon.rs:3156 `rail_migration::migrate_journals_to_rails`) moved the journals under it. cw-rails never rebuilt, so every namespace read empty. The broken contract is the handover's own: rail_migration.rs:4-7, "before any rail surface answers".
  - The handover runs BEFORE any bring-up, in every caller that brings cw-rails up. `ensure_rails` takes the daemon's data dir, resolved through its one existing resolver, and hands over first. Both callers do this: boot.rs, and cli-llm legacy_store.rs `rails_kv()`, since `svrn portfolio` can be the first cw-rails start on an upgraded host. start_daemon's call MOVES; it is never duplicated (principle 8). The in-process EmbeddedDaemon tests keep passing unchanged.
  - When a cw-rails already answers on the base and `<data_dir>/rings` still holds a namespace, the handover moves nothing under the live store. It leaves the namespace in place, and a warn event names the namespaces that wait and says cw-rails must restart to take them (principle 6). `migrate_media_to_rails` follows the same rule.
  - PROOF: a sovereign-daemon process-level test boots the real binary on a temp data dir seeded with a two-namespace `rings/` fixture (NEVER the operator's), with `rails_base` pinned and `CW_RAILS_DIR` set. On the FIRST boot, it reads a seeded row back through cw-rails. PLANT: restore today's order (handover after bring-up) → red. A second test runs cw-rails before the handover: the rows stay at the source, and the warn event names them.
  - Premise: `grep -n 'migrate_journals_to_rails' sovereign/crates/sovereign-daemon/src/daemon.rs` hits once, and `grep -n 'ensure_rails' sovereign/crates/sovereign-daemon/src/daemon_cmd/boot.rs` hits once and runs before `start_daemon`.

A registration names an ALPN, or for `cwth/http/0` a set of path prefixes, plus a loopback port and its declared claims. A second registration of an ALPN or prefix already registered is refused by name, never last-writer-wins (principle 6). The app registry becomes this registry's app entry, not a second one.

  - Rule for every item below: no test in the code closure climbs out of its crate root to find the monorepo (boundary rule 3c's intent; corpus-engine tests/main/source_tree.rs is the precedent). A test reads repo data through a knob, which `.cargo/config.toml [env]` or the loader's own default sets in-tree and the lift's `carry`/`tree` sets in the sandbox. An unset knob panics naming it, never skips (principle 5).
  - The watcher (3 panics, likely 4). A PRODUCT fix, in its own commit with the delta in the body: `IgnoreFilter::is_ignored` (corpus-engine-watchers reindexer.rs:1410) matches `HARD_EXCLUDE` and `extra_ignores` against every component of the ABSOLUTE path, so a project whose root sits under `.cache`, `build`, `dist`, `target`, … is never reindexed. `IgnoreFilter` keeps the root `build_ignore_filter` (:1460) already has, and matches components of `path.strip_prefix(root)` only. A test roots a fixture under a directory named `build` and sees an edit reindexed; PLANT: match the absolute path again, and it goes red. Then re-run code_mcp_e2e.rs:375 in the lift; if it still fails, it is a new finding, census it before fixing.

Keep ONE copy in quality/: backlog-ruler.toml is also read by scripts/co-backlog.py:205, and operational-anchors.toml by sovereign-cli seat_cmd.rs:380, so neither moves beside a crate. `carry` in scripts/lib/program_lift.py (:525) accepts a FILE as well as a directory (copy the file, the knob names the file). `[lift.code]` carries `CO_BACKLOG_RULER = "quality/backlog-ruler.toml"` (the existing knob, ruler.rs:16, env-flags.toml:736). read_notes.rs's mirror test (:625) stops ascending to scripts/sovereign-lint.sh and reads the file through the loader's existing knob `SOVEREIGN_WORKSPACE_DIR` (read_notes.rs:139-141), which the lift carries; no new env var.

Its MECHANISM tests (destination.rs `workspace()` users: a_definition_line…, a_re_exported_destination_resolves, a_name_declared_nowhere…, a_non_type_*, a_disagreement_is_caught…) build a fixture workspace in a TempDir and stop scanning the monorepo. The tests whose subject is THIS repo's live data (every_register_home_matches_the_working_tree, the_repaired_evidence_chain_canonicals_resolve, the_stale_canonical…, refactor_wire negative_control_node_id_fails_with_the_production_bytes) read the tree through `SOVEREIGN_WORKSPACE_ROOT` (set in-tree by .cargo/config.toml) instead of `repo_root_here()`'s git climb. If a live-data test cannot hold on the lift's own tree with `tree = { SOVEREIGN_WORKSPACE_ROOT = "crates" }` (the ingest lift's precedent, program-lift.toml:109), it is a monorepo fact, not the program's (principle 12): move it to the monorepo's gate surface, with its compile and LAYER trial in the body. If no home passes LAYER, write the package.

  - Pre-flight (phase-b-50), the verbs: after the flip, `svrn mesh create` and `svrn mesh join` bring cw-rails up themselves, with the same bring-up `svrn mesh up` runs, so neither starts failing.   - Reboot persistence (operator, phase-b-51): today the node rejoins the mesh at boot because sovereign.service starts the daemon, which is the mesh endpoint. After the flip, nothing starts cw-rails at boot; sovereign.service and sovereign-toolbox.service are the only enabled user units. So `svrn mesh up` installs and enables a cw-rails user unit: the mesh program owns its own restart, as it owns its bring-up (phase-b-31). Neither sovereign.service nor `svrn install-service` (svrn's setup verb) starts or installs it; either would hand one program's lifecycle to another (principle 12). The unit comes from the same writer that `svrn install-service` uses (sovereign-cli-daemon install_service_cmd.rs and what it calls). That writer is shared through host-kit, since it is a mechanism each program's binary needs about itself (§12 3a rung 4), and is never copied into cli-mesh (principle 8). `svrn status` and doctor name the unit's state, so a node off the mesh is reported, never silent (principle 6). PROOF: stop cw-rails, start it through the unit, and status names the node. PLANT: leave the unit disabled, and that check goes red. Reverted.

- [x] HUMAN-pb-mesh-traffic — depends [] — OPERATOR (phase-b-12): how does this node's mesh traffic travel once the daemon stops being an endpoint? The daemon's iroh endpoint (sovereign-mesh iroh_access.rs:648, keyed by `<data_dir>/node_key`) carries peer inference (daemon.rs:3763), guest sessions, offers, the rpc-worker ALPN and published apps (daemon.rs:4355-4378). Every iroh-routed class also dials OUT from it through `RoutedTransport` (daemon.rs:417-444). cw-rails' acceptor serves only its own gossip, media and apps (acceptor.rs:1-35), and its gossip says `inference_capable: false` (gossip.rs:79). pb-mesh-exit-transport, -core and -mesh retire that endpoint, and carrying inference is phase-c's pc-inference-origin, which phase-b-2 deferred as closing no edge; it is in fact on this chain's critical path. Options: (a) RECOMMENDED: pull a narrowed pc-inference-origin into Phase B ahead of pb-mesh-exit-transport. cw-rails' acceptor forwards each of the daemon's traffic classes to its loopback origin, the daemon dials peers through a cw-rails door, cw-rails is the one endpoint, and nothing goes dark. By pb-membership's census that is well over 1,500 lines. (b) The daemon keeps its endpoint and key until phase-c. Nothing breaks, but Phase B cannot close daemon → commonwealth-transport or → sovereign-mesh, so its gate does not reach 0. (c) Accept the gap: peer inference, rpc-worker and guest sessions over the mesh go dark from mesh-exit until phase-c. This row sits LAST in the file, so the loop runs every row that does not wait on it first; only the mesh-exit chain (and whatever depends on it) waits.
  - ANSWERED 2026-09-26 (operator, phase-b-19): option (d). None of (a), (b) or (c). Each class moves with the program that owns it, and cw-rails is the node's one endpoint and forwards to registered origins (pb-rails-origins). (a) made the daemon the origin for weights it no longer holds after pb-svrn-dials-serve (principles 8, 12). (b) fails gate 0. (c) breaks the journeys clause. All three missed the `cwth/http/0` classes.

### Defects found in the 2026-09-25 diligence (REVIEW-pb-census reproduces or strikes each)

Verdicts from REVIEW-pb-census, 2026-09-25. Every one was reproduced by reading at HEAD; none needed a run, and none was struck. Sites are in the owner rows.

- **Hot reload bypasses the engine factory.** On a `kind="remote"` node it loads GGUFs, and under `distributed_primary` it loads the withheld primary in-process (provider.rs:37-62). REPRODUCED: nothing on the path reads `[engine] kind` or `distributed_primary`. Owner: pb-serving-assembly.
- **Daemon `/v1/models` makes a per-request round trip to cw-rails,** which means a self-fact stored in another process and a status produced by a blocking call. REPRODUCED, NARROWED: only the no-local-inference fallback does this (`store_rows`, routes_inference.rs:960). A node with local inference answers from its own manifest (:792). Owner: pb-serve-program, which never takes the path; pb-svrn-dials-serve retires it.
- **GLiNER is loaded twice in one daemon,** with different models when the env var is set (boot.rs:598, runtime-recipe lib.rs:667→832). REPRODUCED. Owner: pb-serving-ner (phase-b-15).
- **The daemon installs a rerank slot that nothing reads;** there is no route, and the recipe opts out. REPRODUCED: no `/v1/rerank` route, and boot.rs:930 passes `AlreadyInProvider`. Owner: pb-serving-kinds.
- **`code_search` silently swaps to full-text search** on an embed error (sovereign-code/src/code_search.rs:123). REPRODUCED. Owner: pb-code-freshness (phase-b-14).
- **`project serve` falls back to an in-memory notes store,** so writes vanish at exit (serve.rs:234-252). REPRODUCED. Owner: pb-code-server.
- **`project init` stamps a model name on zero vectors** (project_init/mod.rs:506-513). REPRODUCED. Owner: pb-code-index.

- **The forced-choice judge fails open on non-sovereign servers,** and the quality probe ignores `SOVEREIGN_DAEMON_URL`. REPRODUCED, SITE CORRECTED. The collapse is core's judge.rs:180, and the fix is on bench's side (live_runner.rs:773 and its callers). The probe is sovereign-cli's quality_check_cmd/exec.rs:31-37. Owner: pb-cli-llm.
- **A `client_daemon_base` parse error becomes the default port.** REPRODUCED (setup_config.rs:1727-1729, `.unwrap_or_else(|_| default_client_port())`), and pc-config-split names it. Owner: phase-c's pc-config-split.
- **`svrn tools list|describe|call` panic** (found by the seat 2026-09-26, after the census): `reqwest::blocking` is built inside the async registry path (mesh_kv_client.rs:45 ← tools_cmd/registry.rs:228), since fp-33. REPRODUCED at fe2edc7bd. Owner: pb-atlas-kv.

### Census baseline (REVIEW-pb-census, 2026-09-25, at b7fbc9909): the §2c drive copy counts later rows report against

- **Bring-up paths: 3.** The decider is `ServingHost::ensure_reachable` (sovereign-turn-client reach.rs:323; production callers in sovereign-cli-mesh mesh_cmd.rs, sovereign-daemon rails_client/bring_up.rs, and the desktop's serving_host.rs and mobile_host_setup.rs). There are two private copies: sovereign-cli-daemon daemon_cmd/lifecycle.rs:581 `start_daemon`'s ready probe, and sovereign-cli serve_cmd.rs:83 `spawn_background`.
- **Sibling-binary locators: 1 shared + 5 private, by name.**
  - Shared: `locate_sibling`, reach.rs:418.
  - Private: sovereign-cli quality_check_cmd/exec.rs:98 `locate_binary`, sovereign-cli serve_cmd.rs:302 `locate_dev_bin_for_spawn`, sovereign-cli-dev drift_cmd_orchestrator.rs:54 `resolve_sovereign_bin`, sovereign-contracts mobile_host.rs:198 `resolve_server_binary` (pb-delete-dead removes it), and the desktop's daemon_binary.rs:66 `daemon_binary`.
  - This is a lower bound from a fn-name grep. phase-b-1's "7 identical sibling locators" came from a different method, and the dispatcher's own sovereign-cli sibling.rs/dev_bin.rs/daemon_bin.rs are the exec mechanism, not counted here.
- **Root locks: 4.** sovereign-contracts run_lock.rs:173 (libc flock), sovereign-core deep_research/state.rs:195 (a second `RunLock`), corpus-engine-scip scip_graph.rs:484 (fs4) and commonwealth-rails lib.rs:154 (std `try_lock`).
- **MCP dispatch loops: 3.** sovereign-daemon mcp_router.rs:408 `dispatch`, corpus-mcp mcp.rs:49 and sovereign-cli-llm mcp_demo_server.rs:102.
- **Tool-set builders: 7 production `ToolRegistry::new()` sites**, with test modules excluded:
  - sovereign-daemon tool_registry.rs:48;
  - sovereign-runtime-recipe lib.rs:547;
  - sovereign-cli-dev project_cmd/serve.rs:389 and tools_cmd/registry.rs:163 (35 `register` calls);
  - sovereign-workflow-host lib.rs:223;
  - sovereign-cli-llm knowledge_gym_cmd/production.rs:247 and recipe_agent_live_trial.rs:1352.
- **Engine assemblies: 3.** sovereign-daemon build/inference.rs:67 `load_provider`, provider.rs:36 `LlamaCppFactory`, and sovereign-compute child_main.rs:354/374/385.
- **Route mounting: 1 daemon mount list of 44 `push`es** (daemon.rs:3547-3699) plus `host_routers`/`host_router_names` (daemon_services.rs:456-487). pb-shell's four adopters each call `axum::serve` themselves.
- **Job execution: 2 paths for ingest.** commonwealth-work `JobExecutorRegistry` (executor.rs:374) with `IngestExecutor`, and the legacy pull loop (auto_ingest.rs:798, driven at :183).
- **bench's closure: 245 crates.** That is `cargo tree -p sovereign-eval -e normal --prune workspace-hack`, 7 of them workspace crates: kernel-types, oicp-types, sovereign-contracts, sovereign-eval, sovereign-time, understanding-vocab and workspace-hack. Without the prune, hakari's unified set makes it 660.
